# Instanced submission scaling qualification

## Decision and scope

Retain the existing renderer. The compatible crate control already submits one indexed-instanced draw from 1,000 through 100,000 visible roots. No production renderer, 112-byte instance layout, material, ordering, Graphics contract, shared counter or shader changes are justified by these measurements. This package adds qualification tests and a dedicated CPU benchmark.

This is a bounded qualification, not final rendering-performance acceptance. Native scale fixtures, isolated ABBA timing, canonical terrain composition, skin load cohorts and the full integration gate remain with their assigned owners. Exact paths follow [Ownership.json](../performance-foundation/Ownership.json); the integrated foundation is master a761969668136f32ce99438e4b6a407094c9567e, version 0.1.530. Its historical false parallelReleased field is conditional on foundation integration, which was verified. No overlapping open PR existed at precheck.

## Source, build and workload identities

Renderer source: a761969668136f32ce99438e4b6a407094c9567e. Qualification checkpoint: e7e1d1a67acfc36e6b8201de22fc75f78db40800 (test commit 36142e18832f032ab143e4d50c3396979322e809). The native client reports 0.1.530-dev+e7e1d1a67acfc36e6b8201de22fc75f78db40800. Final documentation/release metadata receives one PATCH; captured binaries are not relabelled.

Release, .NET SDK 10.0.402/runtime 10.0.13, Windows 26300, x64, 24 logical processors, Intel Core Ultra 285HX. Native adapter: NVIDIA RTX PRO 5000 Blackwell Generation Laptop GPU, driver 32.0.15.9653, 25,309,478,912 dedicated bytes. Software fallback and debug layer are disabled. CPU null-backend samples have no GPU duration.

The unchanged catalog contains 2,055 assets / 94,884,440 bytes, compiler 1.13. Content signature: 135b44877d4882ac539073738a43af7b4794ff35819cc2c1f55c353771743020. Runtime directory fingerprint: 55827DADD0271F6F74F0FB66E2B2774C924C38FC106B6EA351C43BF9A694EBBA. The native manifest independently verifies that fingerprint.

CPU sampler: 128 warmups then 512 chronological frames for each of 12 combinations; three frames per alpha, cycling 0/.25/.5/1 against immutable ticks 31/32, 50ms. Placement uses seed 2026, protocol jitter/grid and Low moves nine of ten roots +4000m in both snapshots. Camera distance 420m, 1920x1080. This CPU-only projection deliberately uses y=0, no terrain rendering, stable ascending identities and no generation churn; it is not the complete [Protocol.json](../performance-foundation/Protocol.json) matrix. BenchmarkDotNet discovers FramesPerTick 1/3/7 through the unchanged assembly switcher; only 3 was sampled here.

Crates load the real compiled industrial crate mesh/LOD/material from the sealed catalog, scale (.1,.12,.1), current translation (.02,0,.03), current yaw .01. Mixed vehicles use the same placement and four production unit definitions at scale (4,3,7), with mechanical metadata. At the selected distance their actual expanded mechanical count is zero; this must not be described as a close mechanical-animation workload. Independent close native cases below exercise expanded parts. No infantry deformation performance claim is made.

## Batch inventory and split reasons

A batch resolves mesh asset/LOD and geometry slice (FirstIndex/IndexCount/geometry-part index, or skin variant), runtime/fallback pipeline and the complete material asset. Binding a runtime material binds base, normal, ORM and emissive textures. Equal input mesh/material handles alone do not establish compatibility. Main and shadow are separate passes with their own pipeline and instance buffer; the shadow pass has its own frustum and root/part/draw budgets.

The same compiled crate is one geometry/material batch, one pipeline bind and four texture binds per nonempty CPU frame. High mixed vehicles resolve four mesh/material pairs and need four draws / sixteen texture bindings. Low selects alternating vehicle kinds because the visibility selector is index modulo ten, so two pairs remain and need two draws / eight bindings. These are workload differences, not a batching improvement. Each retained root occupies exactly 112 uploaded bytes in these non-expanded cohorts. One draw for an entire mixed production scene is not expected.

The new same-mesh building test interleaves normal/critical states with identical input handles, asserts shared resolved geometry/pipeline, two distinct material batches, disjoint instance offsets, exact counts and critical emissive payloads. Four complete texture bindings remain attached to each recorded batch. First-encounter batch order and per-batch source order are preserved.

All 6,144 measured frames report zero current-thread managed bytes, including prepare/record/submit/retire; mean/p50/p95/p99 are all zero. Draw count and upload traffic are constant within each row, so their four distribution values equal the displayed value. Capacity is retained upload storage for one main frame slot, not per-frame traffic. Setup, output row serialization and GPU/native allocations are outside that managed-allocation bracket.

These are descriptive measurements on a shared desktop: build/test work and checkout activity overlapped parts of the run. The complete chronology is retained; tails are not dropped. No speedup, isolated variance bound, FPS target or GPU scaling inference follows. At 100k High the bounded 65,536-root prepared cache yields 65,536 recording hits and 34,464 recording interpolations; at 10k High recording interpolations are zero. That is a measured memory/work tradeoff, not permission to expand the cache without an isolated comparison. Mesh/material resolution and reuse counts stay 1/1 for crates, 4/4 for High mixed, 2/2 for Low mixed, rather than growing with roots. Skin evaluation/reuse/upload counters are zero in these cohorts. No unmeasured per-phase skin or packing duration is substituted for total record time.

## Buffer lifetime and bounds audit

Main and shadow maintain separate dictionaries keyed by the actual admitted FrameIndex. Each upload capacity starts at 64 instances and doubles. Growth creates the replacement before disposing the old wrapper, preserving the old buffer if allocation fails. The device waits for the slot fence before recording. Native upload writes validate recording/in-flight ownership; wrapper disposal routes through GraphicsResourceRetirement and native release waits for the last-use fence. CPU frame modulo is not a completion signal.

Tests verify slots 0/1 with delayed completion, main/shadow storage separation, exact write counts and bytes, completed-slot reuse, replacement/growth and failed-allocation recovery without touching the other in-flight slot. The lane-local fence double rejects a deliberately unadmitted overwrite and recovers after completion. It is not an independent proof of native queue completion; the existing device/retirement contracts remain unchanged.

New paired reference tests sweep rotated nonuniform and negative scales, interpolation alpha, wind metadata, mechanical metadata and three camera distances through main and shadow traversal. Their full instance upload bytes and counts match cluster-culling-disabled reference traversal. Existing spatial tests conservatively retain swept/nonfinite bounds and source order; shadow tests keep whole-root budgets distinct from expanded-part counts. Hidden World/Debug/None filtering is asserted independently for both passes, including zero-visible/zero-draw cases. The compiled test fixture is a minimal triangle asset named through the crate catalog; production crate geometry is exercised by the CPU sampler, not inferred from that unit fixture.

## Native fixed-baseline comparison

The unchanged reference script captured IndustrialBase and BattlefieldCombat, Medium, 1920x1080, CloseTactical/Strategic, Sweep v1, 64 warmups, 256 frames and two independent readbacks per case. Baseline is the retained foundation default capture at 87a14786cb90bff39b33b7edb6069c6dba8b6efc, linked from [Baseline.md](../performance-foundation/Baseline.md). All four PNG and pixel hashes match exactly; all four complete InstanceSubmissionMetrics objects also match. The existing hidden-entity capture assertions pass. Visual/art acceptance remains human review.

| Native case | Visible roots | Expanded instances | Draws | Mechanical instances | Upload bytes | GPU samples | GPU ms mean/p50/p95/p99 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| IndustrialBase-1920x1080-Medium-CloseTactical-Sweep | 34 | 38 | 23 | 7 | 4256 | 239 | 10.986/11.488/15.173/16.535 |
| IndustrialBase-1920x1080-Medium-Strategic-Sweep | 260 | 260 | 40 | 0 | 29120 | 253 | 9.356/9.149/13.702/15.131 |
| BattlefieldCombat-1920x1080-Medium-CloseTactical-Sweep | 32 | 98 | 22 | 69 | 10976 | 254 | 10.287/9.267/16.839/18.076 |
| BattlefieldCombat-1920x1080-Medium-Strategic-Sweep | 258 | 262 | 33 | 0 | 29344 | 254 | 9.724/8.858/15.491/16.112 |

GPU values are actual completed-fence samples at a 1GHz timestamp frequency; sample counts are explicit and lower than 256 where intermediate completed diagnostics were not observed. The manifest retains every CPU frame, GPU fence, resource/health sample, asset-preparation bracket and submission counter. These whole-scene native GPU durations are not 1k/10k/100k object-cohort timings and are not isolated object-pass improvement claims. Native execution overlapped some software-backend resource tests; CPU deltas are retained without attributing them to unchanged renderer code. Pass timings overlap and must not be summed.

## Validation and integration handoff

The full Release solution and dedicated benchmark build pass with zero warnings/errors. The Submission group passes 42 cases (including the new tests); its allocation checks remain enabled. The first full Presentation run exposed missing files in a sparse local checkout. These were restored or populated only after verifying unchanged Git content; no test was relaxed. The final complete Presentation run passes: 2,138 total, zero failures, 67 explicit optional skips (161.955s). Architecture validates 46 projects with no cycles; the five implementation/documentation paths pass the exact ownership validator. Directory.Build.props is changed only at handoff under the serialized version exception: 0.1.530 → 0.1.531.

The original master full build-test has the documented HUD status-marker failure; this package does not repair it or waive the complete CI/terminal gate. Native 1k/10k/100k canonical terrain+objects/terrain-only, High/Low Fixed/Sweep ABBA pairs and skin-specific scale qualification remain unavailable on this base until NativeScalingQualification is integrated. Return those fixtures and any shared-counter/host changes to the serial integration owner. This PR remains a qualification draft until those acceptance items and the complete integration gates are satisfied.

## Reproduction and retained evidence

Run the normal Release build, then the Presentation test executable with -namespace ForgeLine.Presentation.Tests.RenderSubmission. BenchmarkDotNet discovers InstanceSubmissionScalingBenchmarks automatically; FORGELINE_RUNTIME_ROOT optionally selects a sealed runtime catalog. For chronological output call InstanceSubmissionScalingBenchmarks.RunQualification(runtimeRoot, outputPath, sourceRevision, 128, 512) from a local driver referencing the benchmark assembly. The native run uses the unchanged Invoke-RtsReferenceQualification script and the baseline directory above.

Only this document is an assigned new documentation path. Its appendix therefore retains the lossless machine-readable CPU chronology, native candidate/baseline manifests, comparison and validation outputs as gzip/base64, rather than adding unassigned evidence paths. Native images remain in the local capture directory and have the exact hashes of the already retained foundation images. Extract the embedded payload, decode base64, decompress gzip and parse JSON. The envelope records source and input hashes. This is evidence storage, not executable content.

Raw JSON SHA-256: b91afb572fa0a5c36715ab16ebf67f00695ab78270223e2699304f94fff83e49. Raw bytes: 12092849; gzip bytes: 571556.
